: DRESS—PASti lON—HOME. WINTER MILLINERY. As the winter millinery becomes more and - more defined to the types which are to be the faveurites it is obvious, says the Tatler, that we are in for some of the most charming and pic- turesque hats that have been seeff for a very long while. Last year the little toques and very much tilted and exaggerated small hats were rather quaint and effective as seen in the shop windows, and were quite successful on the heads of French and American women, who under- stand the art, of carrying off any marked whim of fashion, but to the average Englishwoman, how- ever smart or well coffee, they were invariably unbecoming and often disastrously so. The Eng- lish girl in a very exaggerated hat invariably looks hopelessly self-conscious and diffident, a hat with a tilt merely looking insecure instead of fascinatingly jaunty. CLOTH COSTUME. Pictured in our 'illustration is a charming little costume in the very latest and most modish style. The material employed is a fine faced cloth, in colour a charming shade known as wood-green. The little bolero which forms the upper part of the costume is cut long enough to completely hide the waistband at the back and sides, and is adorned with a shaped strapping starting from (r"*.... •i: . f ii • 4 Itt f...,,-., , . k , 1 I " I I - t i 111 1 , I r i / 17 , 0 14/ t , ..-.ro c /I 1 \ ~4 I , ,

SWEETNESS AND STRENGTH. Talking machine users, or prospective users—and their name is legion—will be interested in some experi- ments which have been recently conducted with a view of ascertaining the relative wearing qualities of the best known makes of disc records. The most signifi- cant result is seen in the case of the new composition Graphophone discs of the Columbia Phonograph Com- pany which show an average of at least double the wear of any of the others. They are indeed "long life" records, and in this characteristic, together with their musical excellence, lies the explanation of their wide popularity. Longevity, too, is a distinguishing mark of theColurnbia cylinder records. "They wear like iron has become quite a catch expression among users, and their sweet- ness and natdralness are similarly proverbial. Columbia records, cylinder or disc, fit all ordinary talking machines, and sound well on any reputable instrument, but they must be heard on the Columbia Graphophone, with its up-to-date reproducing arrangements, if their musical merits are to be fully appreciated. They then display a brilliancy and purity of tone which is posi- tively charming. Full information about the Columbia Graphophone and records is given in" Price Book 20 " sent post free on application to the Columbia Phonograph Company, General, 64 and 66, Oxford- street, London NV. Particulars of an arrangement whereby old machines of any make are taken in part exchange for new Graphophone models will also be furnished if desired.

POLICE CHANGES. Sergeant John Lings, of Earlestown, is retiring from the Lancashire Constabulary on pension, at the end of the present month. In granting his retirement the Chairman (Mr. W. S. Barrett) expressed the deep regret felt by all sections of the constabulary, that, owing to indifferent health, the county would no longer claim the services of so excellent an officer as Mr. Lings. Ho commentect upon his splendid character, attested by the , Chief Constable, and wished Mr. Lings much happiness and better health in private life. In all probability a local testimonial will be presented to Sergeant Lings, the Vicar, Messrs. T. Bowes, T. Siddelev,and Dr. Patter- son having opened a subscription list for this purpose. —P.C. Mullen has been transferred from Earlestown to Orford, and P.C. Wood of that village has come to Earlestowa. P.C. Powell has removed to Worthington, near Boar's Head. Wigan. His place is taken by P.C. Dawson, of Ice. RUNCORN PETTY SESSIONS. At the Runcorn Sessions on Monday, before Messrs. W. Timmins and D. Bisbrown, John IMulkearn was charged with having used obscene language in Fryer. Street, on November 10th. P.C. Barber proved the case Defendant said his brother-in-law, turned him out at eleven o'clock at night. Why was he not man enough to tell him tine the morning that he did not want him there ? A fine of ss. was imposed. REV. C. F. AIM "CALLED" TO NEW YORK. The Rev. C. E. Aked, pastor of Pembroke Chapel, Liverpool, has been offered the pastorate of the Fifth- avenue Presbyterian Church, New York. He will consult his congregation before taking any steps in the matter. The salary offered Mr. Aked is £5,000 sterling per annum. The Rockefeller family belong to the church. DUKE OF FIFE'S MUNIFICENCE. On Monday the Duke of Fife offered to the Town Councils of Banff and Macduff, as a free gift, Duff House, and that portion of the park immediateiy sur- rounding it, covering an area of 140 acres. The offer was enthusiastically accepted. It is stated that Duff House could not be erected to-day for less than £200,000. The rod-fshing will be very valuable, and the large area of pasture that is part of the gift is studded with noble trees.
